Let's recall LP projection algorithm: Given f (sum fixed sqrt(2n)), M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP max sum g s.t. g≥0, f*g ≤ M. Then rescale g to sum sqrt(2n), update f = (1-t)f + t g. This reduces M. This can be used for any start. If we start from a shape with M<2, the iteration can converge to lower. But solving LP constraints f*g ∞ ≤ M: variables length n, constraints 2n-1 linear inequalities (f * g). For n maybe 256, LP 511 constraints, 256 vars. Specifically, for A=quadratic residues (size (m-1)/2), the number of representations of r as sum of two QR is m/4 + O(√m) (depending on r). Thus pair sums modulo m are nearly uniform. Program design with hybrid:

Potential implementation of fast metric:
```
def fast_objective(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    n = a.size
    # use FFT length at least 2n-1
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2*n-1]
    # numerical small negative? max
    M = float(c.max())
    return (2.0 * n * M) / (s * s)
```
For a binary, F*F not |F|^2; `irfft` of complex with squaring returns real but due numerical may have small imag? irfft expects even-length complex from rfft; F*F may not satisfy Hermitian? For convolution of real sequence with itself, the DFT of convolution is F[0]*F[0], F[k]*F[k] for k>0, and for negative frequencies conjugates? For rfft, the full FFT of length L has F_neg[k]=conj(F_pos[k]). For convolution f*f, full spectrum H[k]=F[k]F[k]. H_neg[k]=conj(F[k])^2=conj(F[k]^2). So the rfft positive part F*F is correct for full spec; irfft reconstructs real. Good.
But note: linear convolution via FFT with zero padding length L: H = F*F. Works.

For exact direct for very small n? Could use `np.convolve` if n small to avoid FFT errors, but FFT fine. For final score exact call evaluate_sequence.

Implementation vectorized perhaps:
```
def subgrad_step(a, step, tol=0.01):
    b=conv_fft(a); M=b.max(); active=np.where(b > M*(1-tol))[0]
    g=np.zeros_like(a)
    n=a.size
    for k in active:
        # derivative b_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}; d/d a_i = a_{k-i} (times 2 maybe) valid
        lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
        # i from lo to hi, partner k-i from k-hi to k-lo
        g[lo:hi+1] += a[hi? partner]
```
Need partner array: for i=lo..hi, j=k-i from k-hi down to k-lo. So `g[lo:hi+1] += a[np.arange(k-hi, k-lo+1)][::-1]` or `np.take(a, np.minimum... )`. If active many (hundreds), loops cost active*n maybe. But step few.
Then update `a -= step*g`, clip 0, normalize sum same? Normalize to keep sum constant: `a *= S / a.sum()`.

Let's define `qr_base(p)`:
```
res = np.zeros(p, dtype=np.int8)
half=(p-1)//2
for i in range(1,p):
    if pow(i, half, p) == 1:
       res[i]=1
# maybe res[0]=0
return res.astype(np.float64)
```
For nonres = 1 - res (excluding0). Include0: res[0]=1.
But computing pow for all i up to 50000 for each p: 50k * maybe log exponent (16) modular multiplications in C? `pow` Python big ints but small; 50k*10 primes =500k pow, maybe several seconds. We can optimize by generating residues as squares: For p, QR set = {i*i % p for i=1..(p-1)//2}. This is O(p) simple modular multiplications in Python (or vector? Python loop 25k). Faster than pow. For p=50000, 25k loops; 10 primes 250k, fine.
```
res = np.zeros(p, dtype=np.int8)
r=1
for i in range(1, p): # i*i mod p
   r = (r * (2*i-1) +1) % p? Simpler: val = (i*i) % p
   res[val]=1
```

Use incremental square: start square=1, for x=1..(p-1)//2: square = (square + 2*x -1) % p? Since (x+1)^2 = x^2 +2x+1. Set res[square]=1.
```
sq=1
for x in range(1, (p-1)//2 +1):
    sq = (sq + 2*x - 1) % p
    res[sq]=1
```
When x=1, sq=(1+1-1)=1 good; x up to (p-1)/2 gives last residues. This O(p/2) Python additions/mults, fast enough.
For higher residues skip.

```
def apply_taper(arr, kind):
   n=arr.size; x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   if kind=='none': return arr.copy()
   if kind=='sin': w=np.sin(np.pi*(x+0.5/n))**2? ensure >0
   if kind=='cos': w=0.5+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*x) # zero endpoints
   return arr*w
```

Functions:
1. `fast_convolve(arr, nfft=None)`: returns conv array length 2n-1? Could just compute in metric.
```
def full_convolution_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*n-1]
```
2. `fast_metric(arr, normalize=False)`:
```
def fast_metric(arr, verbose=False):
    a=np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    B=full_convolution_fft(a)
    M=float(B.max())
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * a.size * M / (s*s))
```
Note: If values all small, metric large; okay. We don't normalize inside. For stochastic with sum normalized, okay.

4. `qr_indicator(p, include_zero=False)`:
```
def qr_indicator(p, include_zero=False):
    res=np.zeros(p, dtype=np.int8)
    if p%2==0: return None
    sq=1
    limit=(p-1)//2
    for x in range(1, limit+1):
       sq = (sq + 2*x - 1) % p
       res[sq]=1
    if include_zero: res[0]=1
    return res.astype(np.float64)
```
Check incremental: x=1 sq=1; x=2 sq=1+3=4; x=3:4+5=9. Good.
For p=3 limit1 sq=1 res[1]=1; QR {1}.
For nonres: `1.0 - res` if include_zero? If exclude zero, set res[0]=0 and nonres[0]=0? If include_zero variant, nonres include zero? We can create:
```
res_excl=qr_indicator(p,False); non_excl=1-res_excl (non[0]=1? Actually 1 - 0 gives1 at 0; undesired. So non_excl[0]=0)
res_incl=res_excl.copy(); res_incl[0]=1
non_incl=(1-res_incl) # non[0]=0
```
We'll produce four arrays: res_excl, non_excl (non[0]=0), res_incl, non_incl.
For non_excl: start res_excl copy, `1-res_excl`, then set [0]=0.
For non_incl: `1-res_incl`.
But note non_excl includes all nonresidues plus? Exclude zero: There are (p-1)/2 nonresidues, zero 0. 1-res_excl gives 1 at zero too; set zero0. Good.

7. `tapered(arr, kind)`:
```
def tapered(arr, kind):
    a=np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n=a.size
    if kind=='none': return a
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    if kind=='sin':
        w=np.sin(np.pi * (x + 0.5/n))
        w=w*w
    elif kind=='cos':
        w=0.5*(1.0 + np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x))
        # endpoints zero, but zero at both ends; add small epsilon to avoid? metric handles zeros, sum positive if interior.
    elif kind=='sqrt_cos':
        w=np.sqrt(np.clip(0.5*(1.0 + np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x)),0,1))
    else: return a
    return a * w
```
Maybe window cos makes endpoints zero; for QR binary maybe okay.

```
def refine_candidate(a, rng, time_limit=20.0):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size==0 or float(a.sum())<0.01: return a.copy(), np.inf
    # Keep original for binary flips separately? We'll do both.
    best=a.copy(); best_score=fast_metric(a)
    cur=a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    # multiplicative perturbations
    for step in (0.25,0.12,0.06,0.03):
       for _ in range(150):
          if time.monotonic()-start > time_limit: break
          a2=cur.copy()
          r=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, a2.size//100)))
          idx=rng.integers(0, a2.size, size=r)
          f=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, step, size=r))
          a2[idx] = np.clip(a2[idx] * f, 0.0, 1000.0)
          s2=float(a2.sum())
          if s2 < 0.01: continue
          a2 *= (float(cur.sum()) / s2)
          sc=fast_metric(a2)
          if sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
              cur=a2; cur_score=sc
          if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
              best=a2.copy(); best_score=sc
          # optional anneal? skip
    # binary flip if original close binary
    if np.all(np.abs(a - (a>0.5)) < 1e-6):
       b=(a>0.5).astype(np.float64)
       curb=b.copy(); curscore=fast_metric(b)
       if curscore < best_score: best=b.copy(); best_score=curscore
       for _ in range(500):
          if time.monotonic()-start > time_limit: break
          b2=curb.copy()
          r=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, b2.size//200)))
          idx=rng.integers(0, b2.size, size=r)
          b2[idx] = 1.0 - b2[idx]
          sc=fast_metric(b2)
          if sc < curscore:
             curb=b2; curscore=sc
          if sc < best_score:
             best=b2.copy(); best_score=sc
       # also try flips from best if binary?
    return best, best_score
```
But `np.all(np.abs(a - (a>0.5)) < 1e-6)` on large arrays okay. For QR with taper not binary.
This refine may spend time. We'll call on top 2 with time left maybe 60s each.

Let's outline code fully:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import operator
import math

TOP_LIMIT=30
MAXP=50000

@helper functions...

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    total = time.monotonic() + max(0, float(budget_s)) - 30
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    top=[]
    # add height_sequence_1
    hsg = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if hsg is not None:
       add_candidate(top, np.asarray(hsg,float).ravel(), TOP_LIMIT)
    primes = primes_up_to(MAXP)
    target_sizes=[997, 2000, 4000, 6000, 8000, 10000, 12000, 15000, 18000, 22000, 26000, 32000, 40000, 48000]
    prime_set=set(primes)
    targets=[]
    for t in target_sizes:
       # find smallest prime >= t
       found=None
       for q in primes:
          if q>=t:
             found=q; break
       if found is not None and found not in targets:
          targets.append(found)
    targets.sort()
    for p in targets:
       if time.monotonic() > total - 120: break
       print(f"trying prime {p}")
       res_ex=qr_indicator(p, False)
       non_ex=1.0 - res_ex; non_ex[0]=0.0
       res_in=res_ex.copy(); res_in[0]=1.0
       non_in=1.0 - res_in
       bases=[res_ex, non_ex, res_in, non_in]
       nshifts = 80 if p<=8191 else (50 if p<=20000 else 30 if p<=35000 else 18)
       shifts=[0]
       if p>1 and nshifts>1:
           shifts.extend([int(x) for x in rng.integers(0,p,nshifts-1)])
       for base in bases:
           for sh in shifts:
              if time.monotonic() > total - 120: break
              cand=np.roll(base, sh)
              add_candidate(top, cand, TOP_LIMIT)
           # tapers
           for kind in ('sin','cos','sqrt_cos'):
              tarr=tapered(base, kind)
              if float(tarr.sum())>0:
                 add_candidate(top, tarr, TOP_LIMIT)
                 if p>1:
                    sh=int(rng.integers(0,p))
                    add_candidate(top, np.roll(tarr, sh), TOP_LIMIT)
       print(f"after p={p} top score {top[0][0] if top else inf}")
    # refine top if time
    refine_limit=60.0
    if time.monotonic() < total - 60 and top:
       candidates_to_refine=top[:3]
       refined=[]
       for sc, arr in candidates_to_refine:
          if time.monotonic() >= total - 60: break
          t_start=time.monotonic()
          refined_arr, refined_sc = refine_candidate(arr, rng, max(0.0, total - t_start - 30))
          add_candidate(top, refined_arr, TOP_LIMIT)
       # Maybe exact a few immediately if time
    # exact evaluation top candidates
    best_es=np.inf; best_arr=None
    # exact candidates sorted by fast, but avoid too many huge if time
    for index,(sc, arr) in enumerate(top[:12]):
       if time.monotonic() > total - 5: break
       # skip if exact very large? No.
       es=exact_score(arr)
       print(f"exact candidate {index} fast={sc:.6f} exact={es:.6f} n={arr.size}")
       if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_es:
          best_es=es; best_arr=arr.copy()
    # if no exact success, use top fast
    if best_arr is None:
       best_arr=top[0][1]
    # Optional: if best_es > target and time left, generate random QR with larger p? loops already.
    out=[float(x) for x in np.clip(np.asarray(best_arr,float),0,1000)]
    print("final exact" , best_es, "len", len(out))
    return out
```
Need `add_candidate` uses fast_metric; if top list passed, sorting with itemgetter. It should not update exact best.
```
def add_candidate(top, arr, top_limit):
    if arr is None: return None
    a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return None
    sc=fast_metric(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return None
    top.append((sc, a.copy()))
    top.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    if len(top)>top_limit:
        top.pop()
    return sc
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import operator


def full_convolution_fft(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a using FFT. Expected to be very fast for n up to ~50k."""
    n = int(a.size)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if need <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 1 << int(need.bit_length())
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    return out[:need]


def fast_metric(arr):
    """
    Fast surrogate for evaluate_sequence().  Same formula:
        2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2
    with the same clipping / positivity protections.
    """
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    if a.size <= 256:
        b = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        b = full_convolution_fft(a)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * float(a.size) * m / (s * s))


def exact_score(arr):
    """Use the provided evaluator if available; fallback to fast_surrogate."""
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.astype(float).tolist()))
        if np.isnan(val) or np.isinf(val):
            return fast_metric(arr)
        return val
    except Exception:
        return fast_metric(arr)


def add_candidate(top_list, arr, top_limit):
    """
    Evaluate arr by fast_metric() and keep the best top_limit arrays.
    top_list contains (score, array) tuples.
    """
    if arr is None:
        return None
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    sc = fast_metric(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return None
    top_list.append((sc, a.copy()))
    top_list.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    if len(top_list) > top_limit:
        top_list.pop()
    return sc


def primes_up_to(n):
    """Naive but adequate sieve for n <= 100000."""
    if n < 2:
        return []
    sieve = bytearray(b"\x01") * (n + 1)
    sieve[0] = 0
    sieve[1] = 0
    r = int(n ** 0.5)
    for i in range(2, r + 1):
        if sieve[i]:
            start = i * i
            step = i
            sieve[start:n + 1:step] = b"\x00" * (((n - start) // step) + 1)
    return [i for i in range(2, n + 1) if sieve[i]]


def qr_indicator(p, include_zero=False):
    """
    0/1 indicator of nonzero quadratic residues modulo prime p.
    Uses incremental squares for speed.
    """
    res = np.zeros(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p <= 2 or p % 2 == 0:
        if include_zero and p > 0:
            res[0] = 1.0
        return res
    limit = (p - 1) // 2
    sq = 1
    for x in range(1, limit + 1):
        sq = (sq + 2 * x - 1) % p
        res[sq] = 1.0
    if include_zero:
        res[0] = 1.0
    return res


def character_indicator(p, d):
    """
    Indicator of the subgroup of d-th powers modulo prime p, excluding 0.
    Empty if d does not divide p-1.
    """
    if p <= 2 or p % 2 == 0:
        return None
    if d < 2:
        return None
    if (p - 1) % d != 0:
        return None
    exp = (p - 1) // d
    ind = np.zeros(p, dtype=np.float64)
    # Could be a bit expensive for large p, so we only use this for moderate p.
    for i in range(1, p):
        if pow(i, exp, p) == 1:
            ind[i] = 1.0
    return ind


def tapered_array(arr, kind):
    """Apply a nonnegative taper to a sequence. Objective is scale-invariant."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    if kind == "none":
        return a
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if kind == "sin":
        # mild interior lift, zero at exact endpoints
        w = np.sin(np.pi * (x + 0.5 / max(1, n)))
        w = w * w
    elif kind == "cos":
        w = 0.5 * (1.0 + np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x))
    elif kind == "sqrt_cos":
        w = np.sqrt(np.clip(0.5 * (1.0 + np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x)), 0.0, 1.0))
    else:
        return a
    return a * w


def is_binary01(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return True
    r = np.rint(a)
    if not np.all(np.abs(a - r) <= 1e-12):
        return False
    return bool(np.all((r == 0) | (r == 1)))


def refine_candidate(arr, rng, time_limit=30.0):
    """
    Light greedy stochastic refinement:
      1) bit flips if the candidate is essentially 0/1
      2) small multiplicative perturbations on a few random indices
    It preserves the best array found and never returns something worse.
    """
    orig = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if orig.size == 0:
        return orig, np.inf
    # Normalize to max 1 to avoid unnecessary clipping.
    mx = float(np.max(orig))
    if mx > 0:
        orig = orig / mx
    best = orig.copy()
    best_score = fast_metric(best)

    # ---- 1) binary bit-flip hill-climbing, if useful
    if is_binary01(orig):
        cur = (orig > 0.5).astype(np.float64).copy()
        cur_score = fast_metric(cur)
        if cur_score < best_score:
            best = cur.copy()
            best_score = cur_score
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        for _ in range(800):
            if time.monotonic() - t0 > time_limit * 0.45:
                break
            b = cur.copy()
            r = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, (b.size // 800) + 1)))
            idx = rng.integers(0, b.size, size=r)
            b[idx] = 1.0 - b[idx]
            sc = fast_metric(b)
            if sc < cur_score - 1e-14:
                cur = b
                cur_score = sc
            if sc < best_score - 1e-14:
                best = b.copy()
                best_score = sc

    # ---- 2) multiplicative small-scale perturbations
    cur = best.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    steps = (0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03)
    for step in steps:
        for _ in range(160):
            if time.monotonic() - t0 > time_limit:
                break
            a2 = cur.copy()
            r = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, (a2.size // 300) + 1)))
            idx = rng.integers(0, a2.size, size=r)
            f = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, step, size=r))
            a2[idx] = np.clip(a2[idx] * f, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s2 = float(np.sum(a2))
            if s2 < 0.01:
                continue
            if np.max(a2) > 0:
                a2 = a2 / float(np.max(a2))
            sc = fast_metric(a2)
            if sc < cur_score - 1e-14:
                cur = a2
                cur_score = sc
            if sc < best_score - 1e-14:
                best = a2.copy()
                best_score = sc
    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for low-value step-function heights.

    Main idea: use number-theoretic 0/1 sequences modulo a prime, especially
    quadratic-residue indicators, whose pair-sum distributions are highly
    balanced.  Ring-shift, complement, taper and light stochastic refinement
    are used to flatten the linear auto-convolution.  A final exact evaluation
    is performed on the best surrogate candidates.
    """
    budget_s = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s - 25.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    TOP_LIMIT = 24
    max_p = 50000

    top = []

    # Include the provided previous best as a candidate.
    hsq = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hsq is not None:
        try:
            add_candidate(top, np.asarray(hsq, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), TOP_LIMIT)
        except Exception:
            pass

    primes = primes_up_to(max_p)
    prime_set = set(primes)

    # A curated list of prime sizes up to ~50k.
    target_sizes = [
        997, 1999, 4000, 6000, 8000, 10000,
        12000, 15000, 18000, 22000, 26000,
        32000, 40000, 48000
    ]
    chosen_primes = []
    for t in target_sizes:
        for q in primes:
            if q >= t:
                if q not in chosen_primes:
                    chosen_primes.append(q)
                break
    chosen_primes.sort()

    for p in chosen_primes:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
            break
        print(f"[search] prime {p}", flush=True)

        res_ex = qr_indicator(p, include_zero=False)
        non_ex = 1.0 - res_ex
        non_ex[0] = 0.0

        res_in = res_ex.copy()
        res_in[0] = 1.0
        non_in = 1.0 - res_in

        bases = [res_ex, non_ex, res_in, non_in]

        # Optional moderate-character subgroups for smaller primes.
        if p <= 12289:
            for d in (3, 4, 5, 7):
                if (p - 1) % d == 0:
                    ind = character_indicator(p, d)
                    if ind is None:
                        continue
                    if ind.sum() > 0:
                        bases.append(ind)
                        comp = 1.0 - ind
                        comp[0] = 0.0
                        bases.append(comp)

        # Shifts: smaller primes get more exploration, huge primes get fewer.
        if p <= 8191:
            n_shifts = 90
        elif p <= 20000:
            n_shifts = 55
        elif p <= 35000:
            n_shifts = 35
        else:
            n_shifts = 24

        shifts = [0]
        if p > 1 and n_shifts > 1:
            extra = rng.integers(0, p, size=max(0, n_shifts - 1))
            shifts.extend([int(x) for x in extra])

        # Evaluate shifted bases.
        for base in bases:
            for sh in shifts:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
                    break
                cand = np.roll(base, sh)
                add_candidate(top, cand, TOP_LIMIT)

            # Tapered versions can sometimes reduce boundary artifacts.
            for kind in ("sin", "cos", "sqrt_cos"):
                tarr = tapered_array(base, kind)
                if float(np.sum(tarr)) > 0.0:
                    add_candidate(top, tarr, TOP_LIMIT)
                    if p > 1:
                        sh = int(rng.integers(0, p))
                        add_candidate(top, np.roll(tarr, sh), TOP_LIMIT)

        if top:
            print(f"[search] after p={p}, best fast score = {top[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Random restarts: if time remains, generate some additional smooth random
    # vectors as a safety net.  These are not expected to beat the algebraic
    # candidates, but they add robustness.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0 and len(top) < 8:
        n_rand = 768
        for _ in range(8):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
                break
            # Low-frequency random cosine mixtures, then nonnegative clip.
            coeffs = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=6)
            k = np.arange(1, 7)
            phases = rng.uniform(-np.pi, np.pi, size=6)
            t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_rand)
            sig = 0.15 * sum(ci * np.cos(2 * np.pi * ki * t + ph) for ci, ki, ph in zip(coeffs, k, phases))
            vec = 1.0 + sig
            vec = np.clip(vec, 0.0, None)
            add_candidate(top, vec, TOP_LIMIT)

    # Refine a few of the best surrogate candidates.
    if top and time.monotonic() < deadline - 90.0:
        refine_n = 3
        for sc, arr in top[:refine_n]:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 60.0:
                break
            # Give each candidate up to ~60s total.
            t0 = time.monotonic()
            remaining = max(1.0, deadline - 60.0 - t0)
            refined, rsc = refine_candidate(arr, rng, time_limit=min(60.0, remaining))
            add_candidate(top, refined, TOP_LIMIT)
            print(f"[refine] refined score fast={rsc:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Exact evaluation of the best few candidates.  Cap total approximate work.
    best_exact_score = np.inf
    best_arr = None
    exact_ops = 0
    exact_ops_limit = 6_000_000_000

    for idx in range(min(12, len(top))):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
            break
        sc, arr = top[idx]
        n = int(arr.size)
        if n <= 0:
            continue
        ops = 2 * n * n
        if exact_ops + ops > exact_ops_limit:
            # Do not drag out final evaluation; the surrogate rank is already reliable.
            break
        # Avoid stacking multiple very large exact convolutions.
        if n > 40000 and exact_ops > 0:
            break
        exact_ops += ops
        es = exact_score(arr)
        print(f"[exact] candidate idx={idx} fast={sc:.6f} exact={es:.6f} n={n}", flush=True)
        if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = es
            best_arr = arr.copy()

    if best_arr is None:
        if top:
            best_arr = top[0][1].copy()
        else:
            best_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)

    out = np.clip(np.asarray(best_arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel(), 0.0, 1000.0)
    out_list = [float(x) for x in out]

    if not out_list:
        out_list = [1.0] * 1024

    print("[final] length =", len(out_list), "exact best =", best_exact_score, flush=True)
    return out_list
```
